`CMakeLists.txt`:

```txt
cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.20)
project(SF33RDSXBRecompo C)

set(CMAKE_C_STANDARD 11)
set(CMAKE_C_STANDARD_REQUIRED ON)
set(CMAKE_C_EXTENSIONS OFF)
set(CMAKE_POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE ON)

if(NOT CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE)
    set(CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE Release)
endif()

add_compile_options(-Wall -Wextra -Werror)
# The same arithmetic on every target: char is signed (as on x86, not as
# on arm64), and no multiply and add is fused into one rounding (clang
# fuses them on arm64), so the game's floats come out the same.
add_compile_options(-fsigned-char -ffp-contract=off)
if(ANDROID)
    # one .data and .bss per file (not the NDK's section per variable), so
    # the port's can be renamed out of the game's state (cmake/decomp.cmake)
    add_compile_options(-fno-data-sections)
endif()
add_compile_definitions(_GNU_SOURCE)

find_package(SDL3 REQUIRED)
find_package(OpenAL REQUIRED)
find_package(Vulkan REQUIRED)
if(NOT ANDROID)
    find_package(OpenGL REQUIRED)
endif()
find_package(ZLIB REQUIRED)
find_package(PkgConfig REQUIRED)
if(ANDROID)
    # android/build-deps.sh's static libarchive and liblzma, in SF_ANDROID_PREFIX
    set(SF_ANDROID_PREFIX "" CACHE PATH "android/build-deps.sh's prefix")
    add_library(PkgConfig::LIBARCHIVE INTERFACE IMPORTED)
    set_target_properties(PkgConfig::LIBARCHIVE PROPERTIES
        INTERFACE_INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES "${SF_ANDROID_PREFIX}/include"
        INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES "${SF_ANDROID_PREFIX}/lib/libarchive.a;${SF_ANDROID_PREFIX}/lib/liblzma.a;ZLIB::ZLIB")
    set(LIBARCHIVE_FOUND TRUE)
else()
    pkg_check_modules(LIBARCHIVE IMPORTED_TARGET libarchive)
endif()

option(SF_USE_MIMALLOC "Use mimalloc for sf_alloc" OFF)

# Shaders: GLSL compiled to SPIR-V at build time and kept as C arrays.
find_program(GLSLC glslc REQUIRED)
find_package(Python3 REQUIRED COMPONENTS Interpreter)
set(SF_GEN "${CMAKE_BINARY_DIR}/gen")
set(SF_SHADER_HEADERS "")
foreach(sh scene.vert scene.frag show.vert show.frag copy.frag)
    string(REPLACE "." "_" n "${sh}")
    add_custom_command(OUTPUT "${SF_GEN}/render/${n}_spv.h"
        COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND} -E make_directory "${SF_GEN}/render"
        COMMAND ${GLSLC} --target-env=vulkan1.0 -O "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/shaders/${sh}" -o "${SF_GEN}/render/${n}.spv"
        COMMAND ${Python3_EXECUTABLE} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/tools/spv2h.py" "${SF_GEN}/render/${n}.spv"
            "${SF_GEN}/render/${n}_spv.h" "sf_${n}_spv"
        DEPENDS "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/shaders/${sh}" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/tools/spv2h.py"
        COMMENT "Shader ${sh}")
    list(APPEND SF_SHADER_HEADERS "${SF_GEN}/render/${n}_spv.h")
endforeach()
# the scene shader again with one texture bound per batch, for a GPU that
# cannot index an array of textures (src/render/scene.c)
add_custom_command(OUTPUT "${SF_GEN}/render/scene1_frag_spv.h"
    COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND} -E make_directory "${SF_GEN}/render"
    COMMAND ${GLSLC} --target-env=vulkan1.0 -O -DSF_ONE_TEX "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/shaders/scene.frag"
        -o "${SF_GEN}/render/scene1_frag.spv"
    COMMAND ${Python3_EXECUTABLE} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/tools/spv2h.py" "${SF_GEN}/render/scene1_frag.spv"
        "${SF_GEN}/render/scene1_frag_spv.h" "sf_scene1_frag_spv"
    DEPENDS "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/shaders/scene.frag" "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/tools/spv2h.py"
    COMMENT "Shader scene.frag (one texture)")
list(APPEND SF_SHADER_HEADERS "${SF_GEN}/render/scene1_frag_spv.h")

if(ANDROID)
    set(SF_GL_SOURCES src/render/gl_none.c)
else()
    set(SF_GL_SOURCES src/render/gl.c src/render/glscene.c)
endif()
add_library(sf_render STATIC
    src/render/renderer.c
    src/render/vulkan.c
    ${SF_GL_SOURCES}
    src/render/scene.c
    ${SF_SHADER_HEADERS}
)
target_include_directories(sf_render PUBLIC src "${SF_GEN}")
target_link_libraries(sf_render PUBLIC SDL3::SDL3 Vulkan::Vulkan)
if(ANDROID)
    target_compile_definitions(sf_render PUBLIC SF_NO_GL)
else()
    target_link_libraries(sf_render PUBLIC OpenGL::GL)
endif()

add_library(sf_import STATIC
    src/iso/xdvdfs.c
    src/iso/archive.c
    src/iso/afs.c
    src/iso/extract.c
    src/iso/xbe.c
    src/host/compat.c
)
target_include_directories(sf_import PUBLIC src)
if(LIBARCHIVE_FOUND)
    target_compile_definitions(sf_import PUBLIC SF_HAVE_LIBARCHIVE)
    target_link_libraries(sf_import PUBLIC PkgConfig::LIBARCHIVE)
endif()


# The tests run where they are built (they spawn tools and write temp
# trees the POSIX way); a cross build for Windows or Android leaves them out.
if(NOT WIN32 AND NOT ANDROID)
add_executable(test_import tests/test_import.c)
target_include_directories(test_import PRIVATE src)
target_link_libraries(test_import PRIVATE sf_import)
target_compile_definitions(test_import PRIVATE SF_SHADER_TOOL="${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/tools/spirv.py")

add_executable(test_shaders tests/test_shaders.c src/render/nv2a.c)
target_include_directories(test_shaders PRIVATE src)
target_link_libraries(test_shaders PRIVATE SDL3::SDL3 Vulkan::Vulkan OpenGL::GL)
target_compile_definitions(test_shaders PRIVATE SF_SHADER_TOOL="${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/tools/spirv.py")

enable_testing()
add_executable(test_frame tests/test_frame.c src/game/task.c src/game/memman.c src/game/opening.c src/mem/heap.c)
target_include_directories(test_frame PRIVATE src)

add_executable(test_picture tests/test_picture.c src/game/fs.c src/game/ppg.c src/game/title.c src/game/pieces.c src/mem/heap.c)
target_include_directories(test_picture PRIVATE src)
target_link_libraries(test_picture PRIVATE ZLIB::ZLIB)

add_executable(test_adx tests/test_adx.c src/game/adx.c src/game/aix.c src/game/fs.c src/game/xwb.c src/game/tracks.c src/mem/heap.c)
target_include_directories(test_adx PRIVATE src)
target_link_libraries(test_adx PRIVATE m)

add_test(NAME import COMMAND test_import)
add_test(NAME shaders COMMAND test_shaders)
add_test(NAME frame COMMAND test_frame)
add_test(NAME picture COMMAND test_picture)
add_test(NAME adx COMMAND test_adx)
endif()

include(cmake/decomp.cmake)

```

`README.md`:

```md
# SF33RDSXBRecompo

Native x86-64 port of Street Fighter III: 3rd Strike, built from the Xbox version, for Linux and Windows.

![The title screen](docs/images/title.png)

The game logic is the C of [3s-decomp-ogxb](https://github.com/Manya3084/3s-decomp-ogxb), compiled for x86-64. Everything the Xbox provided under it is written natively here: SDL3 for the window, input and timing, a Vulkan renderer for the game's draws (OpenGL 3.3 where Vulkan is missing), OpenAL for sound, plain files for the disc archive, and SQLite for the saves. No Xbox code is built. There is no Xbox heap, XDK, xboxkrnl, D3D8, CRI middleware, XACT or XOnline dependency. The XBE is read once, at import, for the sound effects' cue tables; nothing from the disc is in this repository. [docs/porting.md](docs/porting.md) describes how the pieces fit.

## Status

Working:

- the boot logos, the opening and attract demos, the title screen and menus
- Arcade, character select, and fights, including the attract-loop fights
- music: the ADX and AIX tracks, decoded natively and streamed through OpenAL
- sound effects and voices: the game's XACT cue banks played on OpenAL (PCM and Xbox ADPCM waves, loops, envelopes, fades, panning, the pitch LFO's vibrato)
- saves (system settings, button directions, and ten replay slots) in `data/save/game.sqlite`, picked on the game's REPLAY screens
- the keyboard and SDL3 gamepads, with retail's join-by-Start, and a keyboard page in BUTTON CONFIG for rebinding the keys
- windowed, borderless or fullscreen, the resolution, the aspect ratio and the picture filter, set in SCREEN ADJUST, paced to 60 frames a second; the 448-line game area fills the window's height
- Vulkan, or OpenGL 3.3 when Vulkan is missing (`SF_RENDERER=opengl` picks it)
- the game at a locked 60 on one thread, with the drawing on a render thread and the disc reads and sound decoding on worker threads ([docs/porting.md](docs/porting.md#threads))
- EXIT GAME in the mode menu (retail's SELECT GAME), which quits cleanly

Not done yet:

- online play (the game runs offline; Xbox Live is greyed out in the mode menu)

## Download

The [releases](https://github.com/Manya3084/SF33RDSXBRecompo/releases) have a Windows and a Linux package. Neither has any game data: the game imports your own Xbox disc the first time it starts (see [Running](#running)).

**Windows** (64-bit Windows 10 or 11):

1. Download `sf33rdsxb-windows-x86_64.zip` and unzip the whole folder somewhere you can write to, such as Documents or the Desktop. Not Program Files: the game keeps its data and saves next to `sf33rdsxb.exe`.
2. Run `sf33rdsxb.exe`. The program is not signed, so Windows may say "Windows protected your PC": click More info, then Run anyway.
3. Pick your Xbox disc image, zip or 7z in the window that opens.

It needs a graphics driver with Vulkan or OpenGL 3.3. The DLLs it uses are in the folder.

**Linux** (glibc 2.39 or newer: Ubuntu 24.04, Debian 13, Fedora 40 or later):

1. Install OpenAL, libarchive and the Vulkan loader:
   - Ubuntu/Debian: `sudo apt install libopenal1 libarchive13t64 libvulkan1`
   - Fedora: `sudo dnf install openal-soft libarchive vulkan-loader`
   - Arch: `sudo pacman -S openal libarchive vulkan-icd-loader`
2. Unpack `sf33rdsxb-linux-x86_64.tar.gz` and run `./sf33rdsxb` in it. SDL3 is included (`lib/`).

## Getting the data

The game data comes from your own copy of the Xbox disc. `sf33rdsxb` imports it, either on first launch or with:

```
sf33rdsxb --extract SOURCE --data DIR
```

`SOURCE` is one of:

- an Xbox XDVDFS disc image;
- a zip or 7z archive of that disc (the image itself, or the extracted Xbox tree);
- the extracted Xbox folder (`sf33rd.xbe` and `XB_HostPC/SF33RDXB.AFS`).

Anything else is refused, including a PlayStation 2 disc packed in a zip or 7z. The reader looks for the Xbox volume id `MICROSOFT*XBOX*MEDIA` and for `XB_HostPC/SF33RDXB.AFS`. It copies the 3rd Strike archive, the opening AIX, the fonts and the NV2A shaders, and reads the 21 sound banks (the sound effects' cue tables) out of `sf33rd.xbe`. It does not keep the XBE or copy the SF2 movies.

Data imported before the sound banks were can get them on their own:

```
sf33rdsxb --sound-banks /path/to/sf33rd.xbe --data DIR
```

Never commit anything under `data/`. It is the game itself.

## Running

```
sf33rdsxb            (Linux)
sf33rdsxb.exe        (Windows)
```

The first time, the window asks for your Xbox disc:

![The first start: where to get the game data](docs/images/import.png)

- click the window (or press Enter) and pick the disc image, or a zip or 7z of it;
- or press F and pick the extracted disc folder (the one holding `sf33rd.xbe` and `XB_HostPC`);
- or drop any of those on the window.

It imports the game into `data/` next to the program (about 1.3 GB) and starts it. The next time, it starts straight away. Only the Xbox disc works; a PlayStation 2 image is refused with a message saying so. Data imported before the sound effects were read from `sf33rd.xbe` is asked for again.

## Screenshots

| | |
|---|---|
| ![The mode menu](docs/images/mode-menu.png) | ![A fight](docs/images/fight.png) |
| ![Ken's super art](docs/images/super.png) | ![K.O.](docs/images/ko.png) |

## Controls

Players join the way they did on the Xbox: press Start on any pad.

| Xbox     | Keyboard  |
|----------|-----------|
| d-pad    | arrows    |
| A, B     | Z, X      |
| X, Y     | A, S      |
| White    | Q         |
| Black    | E         |
| L, R     | W, R      |
| Start    | Enter     |
| Back     | Backspace |

Those are the keyboard's defaults. In OPTION → BUTTON CONFIG, F1 opens the keyboard page: pick a button with the arrows, press Enter, then the new key (a key already in use swaps over). Esc goes back. The page always uses the arrows, Enter and Esc, whatever the bindings.

The keyboard is port 0. Gamepads take ports 1 to 3 in the order SDL3 reports them, and they are read even when the window is not focused.

## Display

OPTION → SCREEN ADJUST's FILTER row cycles four pictures. Left steps back and right steps on:

- OFF: each game pixel stays a hard edge.
- SMOOTH: the picture is filtered as it is scaled into the window.
- CRT CURVED: scanlines on the 448 game lines, a faint shadow mask, a light curve and a little glow.
- CRT FLAT: that same CRT, with the corners left square.

Three rows follow FILTER:

- WINDOW MODE: WINDOWED, BORDERLESS (the desktop's size) or FULLSCREEN (a mode of the display).
- RESOLUTION: the window's size, or the fullscreen mode. The list is the display's modes and the 4:3 sizes that fit it. Borderless always uses the desktop.
- ASPECT RATIO: 4:3 (the original, with bars at the sides), 16:9 (widened), or FULL (the whole window).

DEFAULT SETTING puts them back to SMOOTH in a 1280×960 window at 4:3. FILTER and the picture size are written to the system database when they change. WINDOW MODE, RESOLUTION and ASPECT RATIO are written to the host database when those rows change.

## Layout

```
data/afs/SF33RDXB.AFS     the disc archive, read in place
data/afs/index            its members: index name bytes
data/afs/files/           the members, unpacked under their stored names
data/audio/ST3_3_OP.aix   the opening theme
data/audio/sdbk/          the sound effects' cue tables, 00.sdbk to 20.sdbk
data/fonts/
data/save/game.sqlite     the system row (includes FILTER), the directions, and ten replay slots
data/last_session.input   the last session's inputs, for replaying a bug
data/crash.log            the backtrace of the last crash
config/host.sqlite        the window and the keyboard
```

A new install creates those databases from the original defaults: the game's factory system and directions, a 1280×960 4:3 window, and the keyboard defaults above. An older ini or memory-card save is not read. SQLite is compiled into the program.

## Build

```
cmake -S . -B build -DSF_DECOMP_DIR=/path/to/3s-decomp-ogxb
cmake --build build
ctest --test-dir build --output-on-failure
```

Requirements:

- SDL3, Vulkan, OpenAL, OpenGL, zlib and libarchive;
- `glslc`, to compile the shaders;
- Python 3, for the build's code generators.

For Windows, cross-built with MinGW-w64 from Linux:

```
cmake -S . -B build-win -DCMAKE_TOOLCHAIN_FILE=cmake/mingw-w64.cmake \
    -DSF_WIN_SYSROOT=/path/to/mingw64 -DSF_DECOMP_DIR=/path/to/3s-decomp-ogxb
cmake --build build-win
```

`SF_WIN_SYSROOT` is a MinGW-w64 root with SDL3, OpenAL, libarchive, zlib and the Vulkan loader (the MSYS2 `mingw-w64-x86_64-*` packages, unpacked). The DLLs the exe needs go next to it.

The Linux release package is built by [.github/workflows/linux.yml](.github/workflows/linux.yml) on Ubuntu 24.04.

`SF_DECOMP_DIR` is a checkout of 3s-decomp-ogxb (default `../3s-decomp-ogxb`). Until it is merged, that checkout must be on its `xbox-port-data` branch. The port is 64-bit only, an ordinary position-independent program with ASLR (see [docs/porting.md](docs/porting.md#x86-64)).

`SF_SCALE=N` fixes the render scale, and `SF_COUNTRY=1..4` picks the region. The debugging switches (headless runs, input replay, frame captures) are listed in [docs/porting.md](docs/porting.md#debugging).

```

`android/app/build.gradle.kts`:

```kts
// The Android app: SDL's activity (its Java, from the SDL checkout
// android/build-deps.sh made: -PsdlJava=PATH) and the game, built by
// android/build.sh into src/main/jniLibs.
plugins {
    id("com.android.application")
}

val sdlJava = providers.gradleProperty("sdlJava").orNull
    ?: error("-PsdlJava=PATH: SDL's android-project/app/src/main/java (android/build.sh passes it)")

android {
    namespace = "io.github.manya3084.sf33rdsxb"
    compileSdk = 37
    ndkVersion = "30.0.16248370"
    defaultConfig {
        applicationId = "io.github.manya3084.sf33rdsxb"
        minSdk = 26
        targetSdk = 37
        versionCode = 104
        versionName = "1.04"
        ndk {
            abiFilters += "arm64-v8a"
        }
    }
    sourceSets {
        getByName("main") {
            java.srcDir(sdlJava)
        }
    }
    buildTypes {
        getByName("release") {
            isMinifyEnabled = false
        }
    }
    lint {
        abortOnError = false
    }
}

```

`android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml`:

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8"?>
```

`android/app/src/main/java/io/github/manya3084/sf33rdsxb/SF3Activity.java`:

```java
package io.github.manya3084.sf33rdsxb;

import android.content.ClipData;
import android.content.ClipboardManager;
import android.content.pm.ActivityInfo;
import android.net.Uri;
import android.os.ParcelFileDescriptor;

import org.libsdl.app.SDLActivity;

/* SDL's activity, with the game's libraries: SDL3, OpenAL Soft, and the
 * game itself (libmain.so, whose SDL_main SDL calls). */
public class SF3Activity extends SDLActivity {
    @Override
    protected String[] getLibraries() {
        return new String[] { "SDL3", "openal", "main" };
    }

    /* The last run's log on the clipboard, to paste into a message
     * (src/port/main.c). Called from the game's thread: the clipboard is
     * the UI thread's. */
    public void copyText(final String text) {
        runOnUiThread(new Runnable() {
            @Override
            public void run() {
                ClipboardManager clip = (ClipboardManager) getSystemService(CLIPBOARD_SERVICE);
                if (clip != null) {
                    clip.setPrimaryClip(ClipData.newPlainText("3rd Strike log", text));
                }
            }
        });
    }

    /* SCREEN ADJUST's ORIENTATION (src/port/display.c): landscape or
     * portrait, held there whichever way the phone is turned. */
    public void setFixedOrientation(final boolean portrait) {
        runOnUiThread(new Runnable() {
            @Override
            public void run() {
                setRequestedOrientation(portrait ? ActivityInfo.SCREEN_ORIENTATION_PORTRAIT
                                                 : ActivityInfo.SCREEN_ORIENTATION_LANDSCAPE);
            }
        });
    }

    /* The file picker's document as a descriptor the game reads the disc
     * through (src/port/setup.c); -1 when it cannot be opened. */
    public int openDocument(String uri) {
        try {
            ParcelFileDescriptor pfd = getContentResolver().openFileDescriptor(Uri.parse(uri), "r");
            return pfd == null ? -1 : pfd.detachFd();
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return -1;
        }
    }
}
```

`android/build-deps.sh`:

```sh
#!/bin/sh
# The Android build's libraries, built from source for arm64-v8a:
# SDL3, OpenAL Soft, xz's liblzma and libarchive (the import's 7z and
# zip). zlib and Vulkan come with the NDK.
#
#   android/build-deps.sh WORK
#
# WORK gets src/ (the checkouts), build/ and prefix/ (what the game's
# build reads: -DSF_ANDROID_PREFIX=WORK/prefix). ANDROID_NDK is the NDK.
set -eu

WORK=${1:?usage: build-deps.sh WORK}
NDK=${ANDROID_NDK:?set ANDROID_NDK to the NDK}
ABI=arm64-v8a
API=26
PREFIX=$WORK/prefix
mkdir -p "$WORK/src" "$WORK/build" "$PREFIX"

fetch() { # url tag dir
    if [ ! -d "$WORK/src/$3" ]; then
        git clone -q --depth 1 --branch "$2" "$1" "$WORK/src/$3"
    fi
}

build() { # dir, then CMake options
    d=$1
    shift
    cmake -S "$WORK/src/$d" -B "$WORK/build/$d" -G Ninja \
        -DCMAKE_TOOLCHAIN_FILE="$NDK/build/cmake/android.toolchain.cmake" \
        -DANDROID_ABI=$ABI -DANDROID_PLATFORM=android-$API \
        -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release -DCMAKE_INSTALL_PREFIX="$PREFIX" \
        -DCMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH="$PREFIX" "$@" >/dev/null
    cmake --build "$WORK/build/$d" >/dev/null
    cmake --install "$WORK/build/$d" >/dev/null
    echo "built $d"
}

fetch https://github.com/libsdl-org/SDL.git release-3.4.18 SDL
fetch https://github.com/kcat/openal-soft.git 1.25.2 openal-soft
fetch https://github.com/tukaani-project/xz.git v5.8.4 xz
fetch https://github.com/libarchive/libarchive.git v3.8.10 libarchive

build SDL -DSDL_SHARED=ON -DSDL_STATIC=OFF -DSDL_TESTS=OFF -DSDL_EXAMPLES=OFF
build openal-soft -DALSOFT_UTILS=OFF -DALSOFT_EXAMPLES=OFF -DALSOFT_TESTS=OFF -DALSOFT_INSTALL_CONFIG=OFF \
    -DALSOFT_INSTALL_HRTF_DATA=OFF -DALSOFT_INSTALL_AMBDEC_PRESETS=OFF -DALSOFT_INSTALL_UTILS=OFF \
    -DALSOFT_INSTALL_EXAMPLES=OFF
build xz -DBUILD_SHARED_LIBS=OFF -DXZ_TOOL_XZ=OFF -DXZ_TOOL_XZDEC=OFF -DXZ_TOOL_LZMADEC=OFF \
    -DXZ_TOOL_LZMAINFO=OFF -DXZ_TOOL_SCRIPTS=OFF -DXZ_DOC=OFF -DXZ_NLS=OFF -DCMAKE_POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE=ON
build libarchive -DBUILD_SHARED_LIBS=OFF -DENABLE_TEST=OFF -DENABLE_TAR=OFF -DENABLE_CPIO=OFF -DENABLE_CAT=OFF \
    -DENABLE_UNZIP=OFF -DENABLE_OPENSSL=OFF -DENABLE_LIBXML2=OFF -DENABLE_EXPAT=OFF -DENABLE_ICONV=OFF \
    -DENABLE_LZ4=OFF -DENABLE_ZSTD=OFF -DENABLE_BZip2=OFF -DENABLE_LIBB2=OFF -DENABLE_ACL=OFF -DENABLE_XATTR=OFF \
    -DENABLE_LZMA=ON -DENABLE_ZLIB=ON -DENABLE_WERROR=OFF -DCMAKE_POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE=ON \
    -DLIBLZMA_INCLUDE_DIR="$PREFIX/include" -DLIBLZMA_LIBRARY="$PREFIX/lib/liblzma.a"

```

`android/build.sh`:

```sh
#!/bin/sh
# The Android APK, start to end:
#
#   android/build.sh [debug|release]
#
# ANDROID_HOME is the SDK (with the NDK in it), SF_DECOMP_DIR the decomp
# checkout (default ../3s-decomp-ogxb), SF_ANDROID_WORK where the
# libraries are built (default build-android-work). The APK is in
# android/app/build/outputs/apk/.
set -eu
ROOT=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")/.." && pwd)
KIND=${1:-debug}
: "${ANDROID_HOME:?set ANDROID_HOME to the Android SDK}"
NDK=${ANDROID_NDK:-$ANDROID_HOME/ndk/30.0.16248370}
WORK=${SF_ANDROID_WORK:-$ROOT/build-android-work}
DECOMP=${SF_DECOMP_DIR:-$ROOT/../3s-decomp-ogxb}
PREFIX=$WORK/prefix

if [ ! -f "$PREFIX/lib/libSDL3.so" ]; then
    ANDROID_NDK=$NDK "$ROOT/android/build-deps.sh" "$WORK"
fi
cmake -S "$ROOT" -B "$ROOT/build-android" -G Ninja \
    -DCMAKE_TOOLCHAIN_FILE="$NDK/build/cmake/android.toolchain.cmake" \
    -DANDROID_ABI=arm64-v8a -DANDROID_PLATFORM=android-26 -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release \
    -DSF_ANDROID_PREFIX="$PREFIX" -DCMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH="$PREFIX" -DCMAKE_PREFIX_PATH="$PREFIX" \
    -DSF_DECOMP_DIR="$DECOMP" -DGLSLC="$NDK/shader-tools/linux-x86_64/glslc" >/dev/null
cmake --build "$ROOT/build-android"

LIBS=$ROOT/android/app/src/main/jniLibs/arm64-v8a
mkdir -p "$LIBS"
cp "$ROOT/build-android/libmain.so" "$PREFIX/lib/libSDL3.so" "$PREFIX/lib/libopenal.so" "$LIBS/"

cd "$ROOT/android"
TASK=assembleDebug
[ "$KIND" = release ] && TASK=assembleRelease
# clean first: an APK packed over the last one keeps the old libraries'
# bytes as dead space
./gradlew --no-daemon -q clean "$TASK" -PsdlJava="$WORK/src/SDL/android-project/app/src/main/java"
ls -l app/build/outputs/apk/*/*.apk

```

`android/gradlew`:

```
#!/bin/sh

#
# Copyright © 2015 the original authors.
#
# Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License");
# you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.
# You may obtain a copy of the License at
#
#      https://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0
#
# Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
# distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS,
# WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
# See the License for the specific language governing permissions and
# limitations under the License.
#
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
#

##############################################################################
#
#   gradlew start up script for POSIX generated by Gradle.
#
#   Important for running:
#
#   (1) You need a POSIX-compliant shell to run this script. If your /bin/sh is
#       noncompliant, but you have some other compliant shell such as ksh or
#       bash, then to run this script, type that shell name before the whole
#       command line, like:
#
#           ksh gradlew
#
#       Busybox and similar reduced shells will NOT work, because this script
#       requires all of these POSIX shell features:
#         * functions;
#         * expansions «$var», «${var}», «${var:-default}», «${var+SET}»,
#           «${var#prefix}», «${var%suffix}», and «$( cmd )»;
#         * compound commands having a testable exit status, especially «case»;
#         * various built-in commands including «command», «set», and «ulimit».
#
#   Important for patching:
#
#   (2) This script targets any POSIX shell, so it avoids extensions provided
#       by Bash, Ksh, etc; in particular arrays are avoided.
#
#       The "traditional" practice of packing multiple parameters into a
#       space-separated string is a well documented source of bugs and security
#       problems, so this is (mostly) avoided, by progressively accumulating
#       options in "$@", and eventually passing that to Java.
#
#       Where the inherited environment variables (DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS, JAVA_OPTS,
#       and GRADLE_OPTS) rely on word-splitting, this is performed explicitly;
#       see the in-line comments for details.
#
#       There are tweaks for specific operating systems such as AIX, CygWin,
#       Darwin, MinGW, and NonStop.
#
#   (3) This script is generated from the Groovy template
#       https://github.com/gradle/gradle/blob/3d91ce3b8caaf77ad09f381f43615b715b53f72c/platforms/jvm/plugins-application/src/main/resources/org/gradle/api/internal/plugins/unixStartScript.txt
#       within the Gradle project.
#
#       You can find Gradle at https://github.com/gradle/gradle/.
#
##############################################################################

# Attempt to set APP_HOME

# Resolve links: $0 may be a link
app_path=$0

# Need this for daisy-chained symlinks.
while
    APP_HOME=${app_path%"${app_path##*/}"}  # leaves a trailing /; empty if no leading path
    [ -h "$app_path" ]
do
    ls=$( ls -ld "$app_path" )
    link=${ls#*' -> '}
    case $link in             #(
      /*)   app_path=$link ;; #(
      *)    app_path=$APP_HOME$link ;;
    esac
done

# This is normally unused
# shellcheck disable=SC2034
APP_BASE_NAME=${0##*/}
# Discard cd standard output in case $CDPATH is set (https://github.com/gradle/gradle/issues/25036)
APP_HOME=$( cd -P "${APP_HOME:-./}" > /dev/null && printf '%s\n' "$PWD" ) || exit

# Use the maximum available, or set MAX_FD != -1 to use that value.
MAX_FD=maximum

warn () {
    echo "$*"
} >&2

die () {
    echo
    echo "$*"
    echo
    exit 1
} >&2

# OS specific support (must be 'true' or 'false').
cygwin=false
msys=false
darwin=false
nonstop=false
case "$( uname )" in                #(
  CYGWIN* )         cygwin=true  ;; #(
  Darwin* )         darwin=true  ;; #(
  MSYS* | MINGW* )  msys=true    ;; #(
  NONSTOP* )        nonstop=true ;;
esac



# Determine the Java command to use to start the JVM.
if [ -n "$JAVA_HOME" ] ; then
    if [ -x "$JAVA_HOME/jre/sh/java" ] ; then
        # IBM's JDK on AIX uses strange locations for the executables
        JAVACMD=$JAVA_HOME/jre/sh/java
    else
        JAVACMD=$JAVA_HOME/bin/java
    fi
    if [ ! -x "$JAVACMD" ] ; then
        die "ERROR: JAVA_HOME is set to an invalid directory: $JAVA_HOME

Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the
location of your Java installation."
    fi
else
    JAVACMD=java
    if ! command -v java >/dev/null 2>&1
    then
        die "ERROR: JAVA_HOME is not set and no 'java' command could be found in your PATH.

Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the
location of your Java installation."
    fi
fi

# Increase the maximum file descriptors if we can.
if ! "$cygwin" && ! "$darwin" && ! "$nonstop" ; then
    case $MAX_FD in #(
      max*)
        # In POSIX sh, ulimit -H is undefined. That's why the result is checked to see if it worked.
        # shellcheck disable=SC2039,SC3045
        MAX_FD=$( ulimit -H -n ) ||
            warn "Could not query maximum file descriptor limit"
    esac
    case $MAX_FD in  #(
      '' | soft) :;; #(
      *)
        # In POSIX sh, ulimit -n is undefined. That's why the result is checked to see if it worked.
        # shellcheck disable=SC2039,SC3045
        ulimit -n "$MAX_FD" ||
            warn "Could not set maximum file descriptor limit to $MAX_FD"
    esac
fi

# Collect all arguments for the java command, stacking in reverse order:
#   * args from the command line
#   * the main class name
#   * -classpath
#   * -D...appname settings
#   * --module-path (only if needed)
#   * DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS, JAVA_OPTS, and GRADLE_OPTS environment variables.

# For Cygwin or MSYS, switch paths to Windows format before running java
if "$cygwin" || "$msys" ; then
    APP_HOME=$( cygpath --path --mixed "$APP_HOME" )

    JAVACMD=$( cygpath --unix "$JAVACMD" )

    # Now convert the arguments - kludge to limit ourselves to /bin/sh
    for arg do
        if
            case $arg in                                #(
              -*)   false ;;                            # don't mess with options #(
              /?*)  t=${arg#/} t=/${t%%/*}              # looks like a POSIX filepath
                    [ -e "$t" ] ;;                      #(
              *)    false ;;
            esac
        then
            arg=$( cygpath --path --ignore --mixed "$arg" )
        fi
        # Roll the args list around exactly as many times as the number of
        # args, so each arg winds up back in the position where it started, but
        # possibly modified.
        #
        # NB: a `for` loop captures its iteration list before it begins, so
        # changing the positional parameters here affects neither the number of
        # iterations, nor the values presented in `arg`.
        shift                   # remove old arg
        set -- "$@" "$arg"      # push replacement arg
    done
fi


# Add default JVM options here. You can also use JAVA_OPTS and GRADLE_OPTS to pass JVM options to this script.
DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS='"-Xmx64m" "-Xms64m"'

# Collect all arguments for the java command:
#   * DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS, JAVA_OPTS, and optsEnvironmentVar are not allowed to contain shell fragments,
#     and any embedded shellness will be escaped.
#   * For example: A user cannot expect ${Hostname} to be expanded, as it is an environment variable and will be
#     treated as '${Hostname}' itself on the command line.

set -- \
        "-Dorg.gradle.appname=$APP_BASE_NAME" \
        -jar "$APP_HOME/gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.jar" \
        "$@"

# Stop when "xargs" is not available.
if ! command -v xargs >/dev/null 2>&1
then
    die "xargs is not available"
fi

# Use "xargs" to parse quoted args.
#
# With -n1 it outputs one arg per line, with the quotes and backslashes removed.
#
# In Bash we could simply go:
#
#   readarray ARGS < <( xargs -n1 <<<"$var" ) &&
#   set -- "${ARGS[@]}" "$@"
#
# but POSIX shell has neither arrays nor command substitution, so instead we
# post-process each arg (as a line of input to sed) to backslash-escape any
# character that might be a shell metacharacter, then use eval to reverse
# that process (while maintaining the separation between arguments), and wrap
# the whole thing up as a single "set" statement.
#
# This will of course break if any of these variables contains a newline or
# an unmatched quote.
#

eval "set -- $(
        printf '%s\n' "$DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS $JAVA_OPTS $GRADLE_OPTS" |
        xargs -n1 |
        sed ' s~[^-[:alnum:]+,./:=@_]~\\&~g; ' |
        tr '\n' ' '
    )" '"$@"'

exec "$JAVACMD" "$@"

```

`android/gradlew.bat`:

```bat
@rem
@rem Copyright 2015 the original author or authors.
@rem
@rem Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License");
@rem you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.
@rem You may obtain a copy of the License at
@rem
@rem      https://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0
@rem
@rem Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
@rem distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS,
@rem WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
@rem See the License for the specific language governing permissions and
@rem limitations under the License.
@rem
@rem SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
@rem

@if "%DEBUG%"=="" @echo off
@rem ##########################################################################
@rem
@rem  gradlew startup script for Windows
@rem
@rem ##########################################################################

@rem Set local scope for the variables, and ensure extensions are enabled
setlocal EnableExtensions

@rem Catch executions from older scripts and ensure they exit cleanly.
@rem This can be removed once we can be reasonably confident that few people
@rem will be migrating directly to this new wrapper.
goto afterSafetyNet
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
goto exitWithErrorLevel
:afterSafetyNet

set DIRNAME=%~dp0
if "%DIRNAME%"=="" set DIRNAME=.
@rem This is normally unused
set APP_BASE_NAME=%~n0
set APP_HOME=%DIRNAME%

@rem Resolve any "." and ".." in APP_HOME to make it shorter.
for %%i in ("%APP_HOME%") do set APP_HOME=%%~fi

@rem Add default JVM options here. You can also use JAVA_OPTS and GRADLE_OPTS to pass JVM options to this script.
set DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS="-Xmx64m" "-Xms64m"

@rem Find java.exe
if defined JAVA_HOME goto findJavaFromJavaHome

set JAVA_EXE=java.exe
%JAVA_EXE% -version >NUL 2>&1
if %ERRORLEVEL% equ 0 goto execute

1>&2 echo.
1>&2 echo ERROR: JAVA_HOME is not set and no 'java' command could be found in your PATH.
1>&2 echo.
1>&2 echo Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the
1>&2 echo location of your Java installation.

"%COMSPEC%" /c exit 1
goto exitWithErrorLevel

:findJavaFromJavaHome
set JAVA_HOME=%JAVA_HOME:"=%
set JAVA_EXE=%JAVA_HOME%/bin/java.exe

if exist "%JAVA_EXE%" goto execute

1>&2 echo.
1>&2 echo ERROR: JAVA_HOME is set to an invalid directory: %JAVA_HOME%
1>&2 echo.
1>&2 echo Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the
1>&2 echo location of your Java installation.

"%COMSPEC%" /c exit 1
goto exitWithErrorLevel

:execute
@rem Setup the command line



@rem Execute gradlew
@rem endlocal doesn't take effect until after the line is parsed and variables are expanded
@rem which allows us to clear the local environment before executing the java command
endlocal & "%JAVA_EXE%" %DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS% %JAVA_OPTS% %GRADLE_OPTS% "-Dorg.gradle.appname=%APP_BASE_NAME%" -jar "%APP_HOME%\gradle\wrapper\gradle-wrapper.jar" %* & call :exitWithErrorLevel & goto exitWithErrorLevel

@rem This label must not be changed. We rely on old scripts being able to jump to this point.
:exitWithErrorLevel
@rem Use "%COMSPEC%" /c exit to allow operators to work properly in scripts
"%COMSPEC%" /c exit %ERRORLEVEL%
```

`cmake/decomp-overlay.txt`:

```txt
# Decomp files that compile from a changed copy for x86-64 (see cmake/decomp.cmake).
# Each change keeps the MSVC relink as it is and belongs upstream in time.
#   patch PATH    apply cmake/decomp-overlay/<PATH with / as _>.patch

# The port's own changes, which stay here (not for the decomp): SCREEN
# ADJUST's WINDOW MODE, RESOLUTION and ASPECT RATIO rows. menu.c runs ten
# rows, EFF61.c has their labels, EFF63.c asks src/port/display.c for their
# values, and Sel_Data.c places the ten rows closer together. FILTER cycles
# OFF, SMOOTH, CRT CURVED and CRT FLAT, and that choice and the picture
# size are written when they change. The window rows write on their own
# step. The mode
# menu's Xbox Live is greyed and skipped, as VERSUS is without a second
# player (menu.c's cursor, EFF61.c's label): the port has no online play.
patch src/sf33rd/Source/Game/menu.c
patch src/sf33rd/Source/Game/EFF61.c
patch src/sf33rd/Source/Game/EFF63.c
patch src/sf33rd/Source/Game/Sel_Data.c

```

`cmake/decomp-overlay/src_sf33rd_Source_Game_EFF61.c.patch`:

```patch
--- a/EFF61.c
+++ b/EFF61.c
@@ -55,7 +55,8 @@
```

`cmake/decomp.cmake`:

```cmake
# The game code: the C of 3s-decomp-ogxb, compiled for x86-64.
#
# The decomp checkout is read, never written. Its include/ is copied into
# the build tree so that types.h can give size_t and the pointer-sized
# integers their LP64 widths; every source file compiles from the checkout
# as it is, apart from the few listed in cmake/decomp-overlay.txt, which
# compile from a patched copy.
#
# Every address the game keeps is pointer-sized, so the program is an
# ordinary PIE with ASLR, and its memory can be anywhere.

set(SF_DECOMP_DIR "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/../3s-decomp-ogxb" CACHE PATH "3s-decomp-ogxb checkout")
get_filename_component(SF_DECOMP_DIR "${SF_DECOMP_DIR}" ABSOLUTE)

if(NOT EXISTS "${SF_DECOMP_DIR}/src/sf33rd/Source/Game")
    message(STATUS "3s-decomp-ogxb not found at ${SF_DECOMP_DIR}; sf_decomp is not built")
    return()
endif()
message(STATUS "3s-decomp-ogxb: ${SF_DECOMP_DIR}")

set(SF_DECOMP_GEN "${CMAKE_BINARY_DIR}/decomp")

# include/, with 64-bit sizes.
file(GLOB_RECURSE _inc RELATIVE "${SF_DECOMP_DIR}/include" "${SF_DECOMP_DIR}/include/*.h")
foreach(h IN LISTS _inc)
    configure_file("${SF_DECOMP_DIR}/include/${h}" "${SF_DECOMP_GEN}/include/${h}" COPYONLY)
endforeach()
file(READ "${SF_DECOMP_GEN}/include/types.h" _types)
# the compiler's own pointer-sized types: long on Linux (LP64), long long
# on Windows (LLP64)
set(_lp64 "typedef __INTPTR_TYPE__ intptr_t;\ntypedef __UINTPTR_TYPE__ uintptr_t;\ntypedef __SIZE_TYPE__ size_t;\ntypedef __PTRDIFF_TYPE__ ssize_t;")
string(REPLACE "typedef s32 intptr_t;\ntypedef u32 uintptr_t;\ntypedef u32 size_t;\ntypedef s32 ssize_t;" "${_lp64}" _types64 "${_types}")
if(_types64 STREQUAL _types)
    message(FATAL_ERROR "decomp include/types.h: the 32-bit size typedefs moved; update cmake/decomp.cmake")
endif()
file(WRITE "${SF_DECOMP_GEN}/include/types.h" "${_types64}")

# Sources: the game logic only. Nothing of the Xbox build is compiled:
# not the platform layer and Xbox-only game code under Source/XBOX (its
# effect draws, save menu and quad renderers are written natively in
# src/), not CRI's Xbox middleware (src/game/adx.c and aix.c decode
# natively), not the PS2 LZ77 (the disc uses zlib), and not the decomp's
# zlib copy (the system's is used).
file(GLOB_RECURSE _src RELATIVE "${SF_DECOMP_DIR}" CONFIGURE_DEPENDS "${SF_DECOMP_DIR}/src/*.c")
list(FILTER _src EXCLUDE REGEX "^src/sf33rd/Source/XBOX/")
list(FILTER _src EXCLUDE REGEX "^src/cri/")
list(FILTER _src EXCLUDE REGEX "^src/sf33rd/Source/Compress/zlib/")
list(FILTER _src EXCLUDE REGEX "^src/sf33rd/Source/Compress/Lz77/")
# zlibApp.c declares a 32-bit z_stream; src/port/zlib.c keeps its API
list(REMOVE_ITEM _src "src/sf33rd/Source/Compress/zlibApp.c")
# MemMan.c keeps two pointers in a cell header that must fit the 16-byte
# unit; src/game/memman.c is the same heap with 32-bit links
list(REMOVE_ITEM _src "src/sf33rd/Source/Common/MemMan.c")

# A file listed in cmake/decomp-overlay.txt compiles from a copy in the
# build tree: "patch PATH" applies cmake/decomp-overlay/<PATH with _>.patch.
# The copy keeps the original's directory on its include path.
find_package(Python3 REQUIRED COMPONENTS Interpreter)
set(_overlay_dir "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/cmake/decomp-overlay")
file(STRINGS "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/cmake/decomp-overlay.txt" _overlay REGEX "^[a-z]+ ")
set(SF_DECOMP_SOURCES "")
foreach(s IN LISTS _src)
    set(_how "")
    foreach(line IN LISTS _overlay)
        string(REGEX MATCH "^([a-z]+) (.*)$" _m "${line}")
        if(CMAKE_MATCH_2 STREQUAL s)
            set(_how "${CMAKE_MATCH_1}")
        endif()
    endforeach()
    set(_in "${SF_DECOMP_DIR}/${s}")
    set(_out "${SF_DECOMP_GEN}/overlay/${s}")
    get_filename_component(_odir "${_out}" DIRECTORY)
    get_filename_component(_idir "${_in}" DIRECTORY)
    string(REPLACE "/" "_" _p "${s}")
    if(_how STREQUAL "")
        list(APPEND SF_DECOMP_SOURCES "${_in}")
        continue()
    elseif(_how STREQUAL "patch")
        add_custom_command(OUTPUT "${_out}"
            COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND} -E make_directory "${_odir}"
            COMMAND ${CMAKE_COMMAND} -E copy "${_in}" "${_out}"
            COMMAND patch --quiet --forward "${_out}" "${_overlay_dir}/${_p}.patch"
            DEPENDS "${_in}" "${_overlay_dir}/${_p}.patch"
            COMMENT "Overlay ${s}")
    else()
        message(FATAL_ERROR "cmake/decomp-overlay.txt: unknown kind ${_how} for ${s}")
    endif()
    set_source_files_properties("${_out}" PROPERTIES INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES "${_idir}")
    list(APPEND SF_DECOMP_SOURCES "${_out}")
endforeach()

add_library(sf_decomp STATIC ${SF_DECOMP_SOURCES})
set_target_properties(sf_decomp PROPERTIES C_STANDARD 17 C_EXTENSIONS ON POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE ON)
target_include_directories(sf_decomp PUBLIC "${SF_DECOMP_GEN}/include")
# The decomp is C written to match retail code, not to pass -Wall; the
# runtime's warnings stay on its own files.
target_compile_options(sf_decomp PRIVATE -g
    -w -Wno-error -fcommon -fno-strict-aliasing -fwrapv
    -Wno-incompatible-pointer-types -Wno-int-conversion -Wno-implicit-function-declaration
    -Wno-implicit-int -Wno-return-mismatch)

# The game: the decomp's game logic on the native platform layer in
# src/port. stubs.c is generated: one logging stub for each name the game
# logic calls that src/port does not define yet, so the list of stubs is
# the native work left.
# The native platform the game logic runs on. sf_gfx is the graphics
# calls (tested on their own, tests/test_scene.c); sf_port the game's
# frame and window.
add_library(sf_gfx STATIC
    src/port/missing.c
    src/port/arena.c
    src/port/gfx.c
)
target_include_directories(sf_gfx PUBLIC src "${SF_DECOMP_GEN}/include")
set_target_properties(sf_gfx PROPERTIES POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE ON)

# SQLite is compiled in (public domain amalgamation) so the packages do
# not gain a DLL. Its globals are renamed with the port's, and a rollback
# snapshot leaves them out.
set(SF_SQLITE_ZIP "${CMAKE_BINARY_DIR}/sqlite-amalgamation-3530400.zip")
set(SF_SQLITE_SRC "${CMAKE_BINARY_DIR}/sqlite-amalgamation-3530400")
if(NOT EXISTS "${SF_SQLITE_SRC}/sqlite3.c")
    file(DOWNLOAD https://sqlite.org/2026/sqlite-amalgamation-3530400.zip "${SF_SQLITE_ZIP}"
        EXPECTED_HASH SHA256=1e71ddf93849c6a6ecf58b827c0692073d2dd7ee40196158068f7b29f422e87d)
    file(ARCHIVE_EXTRACT INPUT "${SF_SQLITE_ZIP}" DESTINATION "${CMAKE_BINARY_DIR}")
endif()
find_package(Threads REQUIRED)
add_library(sf_sqlite STATIC "${SF_SQLITE_SRC}/sqlite3.c")
target_include_directories(sf_sqlite PUBLIC "${SF_SQLITE_SRC}")
target_compile_definitions(sf_sqlite PRIVATE SQLITE_OMIT_LOAD_EXTENSION SQLITE_DQS=0)
target_compile_options(sf_sqlite PRIVATE -w -fno-strict-aliasing)
target_link_libraries(sf_sqlite PUBLIC Threads::Threads m)
set_target_properties(sf_sqlite PROPERTIES C_EXTENSIONS ON POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE ON)

add_library(sf_port STATIC
    src/port/frame.c
    src/port/video.c
    src/port/system.c
    src/port/zlib.c
    src/port/files.c
    src/port/input.c
    src/port/store.c
    src/port/save.c
    src/port/banks.c
    src/port/math.c
    src/port/jobs.c
    src/port/sound.c
    src/port/se.c
    src/port/menu_ext.c
    src/port/display.c
    src/port/state.c
    src/port/setup.c
    src/ui/ui.c
    src/port/live.c
    src/game/adx.c
    src/game/aix.c
    src/game/xwb.c
    src/host/paths.c
    src/game/memman.c
    src/mem/heap.c
)
target_link_libraries(sf_port PUBLIC ZLIB::ZLIB OpenAL::OpenAL sf_sqlite)
target_link_libraries(sf_port PUBLIC sf_gfx sf_render sf_import)
set_target_properties(sf_port PROPERTIES POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE ON)

# The port's own variables (the window, textures, sound, input devices)
# are not the game's state: their sections are renamed so the linker marks
# them (__start_sfport_data and the rest) and a game-state snapshot
# (src/port/state.c) leaves them out.
if(WIN32)
    find_program(SF_OBJCOPY NAMES x86_64-w64-mingw32-objcopy REQUIRED)
elseif(ANDROID)
    set(SF_OBJCOPY "${CMAKE_OBJCOPY}") # the NDK's llvm-objcopy
else()
    find_program(SF_OBJCOPY NAMES objcopy REQUIRED)
endif()
foreach(_lib sf_port sf_gfx sf_render sf_sqlite)
    add_custom_command(TARGET ${_lib} POST_BUILD
        COMMAND ${SF_OBJCOPY} --rename-section .data=sfport_data --rename-section .bss=sfport_bss $<TARGET_FILE:${_lib}>
        COMMENT "Port state sections in ${_lib}")
endforeach()

# Windows: the MinGW tools, and the import libraries whose names the
# system gives (the C runtime, kernel32, OpenGL, and the DLLs it ships with)
set(_stub_win "")
if(WIN32)
    set(_stub_win --coff --nm x86_64-w64-mingw32-nm --objdump x86_64-w64-mingw32-objdump)
    foreach(_l libmsvcrt.a libmingwex.a libmingw32.a libmoldname.a libkernel32.a libuser32.a libopengl32.a libgcc.a)
        execute_process(COMMAND ${CMAKE_C_COMPILER} -print-file-name=${_l} OUTPUT_VARIABLE _p OUTPUT_STRIP_TRAILING_WHITESPACE)
        list(APPEND _stub_win --host "${_p}")
    endforeach()
    foreach(_l libSDL3.dll.a libvulkan-1.dll.a libopenal.dll.a libz.dll.a libarchive.dll.a)
        list(APPEND _stub_win --host "${SF_WIN_SYSROOT}/lib/${_l}")
    endforeach()
endif()
# Android: the NDK's tools, the platform's libraries (in the NDK's sysroot,
# for the API level built for), and the static libarchive and liblzma
set(_stub_archives "")
if(ANDROID)
    set(_stub_win --arch aarch64 --no-system --nm "${CMAKE_NM}" --objdump "${CMAKE_OBJDUMP}")
    file(GLOB _ndk_libs "${CMAKE_SYSROOT}/usr/lib/${CMAKE_LIBRARY_ARCHITECTURE}/${ANDROID_PLATFORM_LEVEL}/*.so")
    foreach(_l ${_ndk_libs})
        list(APPEND _stub_win --host-so "${_l}")
    endforeach()
    list(APPEND _stub_archives "${SF_ANDROID_PREFIX}/lib/libarchive.a" "${SF_ANDROID_PREFIX}/lib/liblzma.a")
endif()
# Linux: the shared libraries the program links, where CMake found them
# (SDL3 built into a prefix, say), as well as the system's own
if(NOT WIN32)
    if(TARGET SDL3::SDL3-shared)
        list(APPEND _stub_win --host-so $<TARGET_FILE:SDL3::SDL3-shared>)
    endif()
    foreach(_l ${OPENAL_LIBRARY} ${Vulkan_LIBRARY} ${ZLIB_LIBRARIES} ${LIBARCHIVE_LINK_LIBRARIES})
        if(_l MATCHES "\\.so")
            list(APPEND _stub_win --host-so "${_l}")
        endif()
    endforeach()
endif()

# The aliases for calls to the bodies retail's linker folded, from the
# port's own archives and the decomp's symbol lists (no Xbox build needed)
if(WIN32)
    set(_fold_nm --nm x86_64-w64-mingw32-nm)
elseif(ANDROID)
    set(_fold_nm --nm "${CMAKE_NM}")
else()
    set(_fold_nm "")
endif()
add_custom_command(OUTPUT "${SF_DECOMP_GEN}/fold.txt"
    COMMAND ${Python3_EXECUTABLE} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/tools/fold_aliases.py" ${_fold_nm} "${SF_DECOMP_DIR}"
        "${SF_DECOMP_GEN}/fold.txt" $<TARGET_FILE:sf_decomp> $<TARGET_FILE:sf_port> $<TARGET_FILE:sf_gfx>
        $<TARGET_FILE:sf_render>
    DEPENDS sf_decomp sf_port sf_gfx sf_render "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/tools/fold_aliases.py"
    COMMENT "Folded-body aliases")

add_custom_command(OUTPUT "${SF_DECOMP_GEN}/stubs.c"
    COMMAND ${Python3_EXECUTABLE} "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/tools/gen_stubs.py" ${_stub_win}
        --aliases "${SF_DECOMP_GEN}/fold.txt" --sizes "${SF_DECOMP_DIR}/config/sf33rd/symbols/data.txt"
        "${SF_DECOMP_GEN}/stubs.c"
        $<TARGET_FILE:sf_decomp> $<TARGET_FILE:sf_port> $<TARGET_FILE:sf_gfx> $<TARGET_FILE:sf_render>
        $<TARGET_FILE:sf_import> $<TARGET_FILE:sf_sqlite> ${_stub_archives}
    DEPENDS sf_decomp sf_port sf_gfx sf_render sf_import sf_sqlite "${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/tools/gen_stubs.py"
        "${SF_DECOMP_DIR}/config/sf33rd/symbols/data.txt"
        "${SF_DECOMP_GEN}/fold.txt"
    COMMENT "Port stubs")

if(ANDROID)
    # libmain.so, which SDL's Android activity loads and calls SDL_main in
    add_library(sf33rdsxb_game SHARED src/port/main.c src/host/paths.c "${SF_DECOMP_GEN}/stubs.c")
    set_target_properties(sf33rdsxb_game PROPERTIES OUTPUT_NAME main)
else()
    add_executable(sf33rdsxb_game src/port/main.c src/host/paths.c "${SF_DECOMP_GEN}/stubs.c")
    # one program: the game, which imports the disc the first time it starts
    set_target_properties(sf33rdsxb_game PROPERTIES OUTPUT_NAME sf33rdsxb)
endif()
target_include_directories(sf33rdsxb_game PRIVATE src)
set_target_properties(sf33rdsxb_game PROPERTIES POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE ON)
set_source_files_properties("${SF_DECOMP_GEN}/stubs.c" PROPERTIES COMPILE_OPTIONS "-Wno-error;-w;-fno-builtin")
if(WIN32)
    # A Windows program with no console.
    target_link_options(sf33rdsxb_game PRIVATE -mwindows -static-libgcc -Wl,-Map=${CMAKE_BINARY_DIR}/sf33rdsxb_game.map)
    target_link_libraries(sf33rdsxb_game PRIVATE sf_port sf_gfx sf_decomp sf_port sf_gfx sf_render SDL3::SDL3 ZLIB::ZLIB)
elseif(ANDROID)
    target_link_options(sf33rdsxb_game PRIVATE -Wl,-Map=${CMAKE_BINARY_DIR}/sf33rdsxb_game.map)
    target_link_libraries(sf33rdsxb_game PRIVATE sf_port sf_gfx sf_decomp sf_port sf_gfx sf_render SDL3::SDL3 ZLIB::ZLIB m log)
else()
    target_link_options(sf33rdsxb_game PRIVATE -pie -rdynamic -Wl,-Map=${CMAKE_BINARY_DIR}/sf33rdsxb_game.map)
    # the port libraries twice: the decomp calls into them, and they into it
    target_link_libraries(sf33rdsxb_game PRIVATE sf_port sf_gfx sf_decomp sf_port sf_gfx sf_render SDL3::SDL3 ZLIB::ZLIB m)
endif()

if(NOT WIN32 AND NOT ANDROID)
add_executable(test_scene tests/test_scene.c)
target_link_libraries(test_scene PRIVATE sf_gfx sf_render)
add_test(NAME scene COMMAND test_scene)

add_executable(test_store tests/test_store.c src/port/store.c)
target_include_directories(test_store PRIVATE src "${SF_DECOMP_GEN}/include")
target_link_libraries(test_store PRIVATE sf_sqlite)
add_test(NAME store COMMAND test_store)
set(_lvp "")
foreach(f /usr/share/vulkan/icd.d/lvp_icd.x86_64.json /usr/share/vulkan/icd.d/lvp_icd.json)
    if(EXISTS "${f}" AND _lvp STREQUAL "")
        set(_lvp "${f}")
    endif()
endforeach()
# lavapipe when it is installed, so the test does not depend on the GPU
if(_lvp)
    set_tests_properties(scene PROPERTIES ENVIRONMENT "VK_DRIVER_FILES=${_lvp};VK_ICD_FILENAMES=${_lvp}")
endif()
set_tests_properties(scene PROPERTIES SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77)
endif()

```

`cmake/mingw-w64.cmake`:

```cmake
# Cross-compiling for 64-bit Windows with MinGW-w64:
#     cmake -S . -B build-win -DCMAKE_TOOLCHAIN_FILE=cmake/mingw-w64.cmake \
#           -DSF_WIN_SYSROOT=/path/to/root/mingw64 -DSF_DECOMP_DIR=...
# SF_WIN_SYSROOT holds the Windows builds of SDL3, OpenAL Soft, libarchive,
# zlib and the Vulkan loader (MSYS2's mingw-w64-x86_64 packages, unpacked).
set(CMAKE_SYSTEM_NAME Windows)
set(CMAKE_SYSTEM_PROCESSOR x86_64)
set(CMAKE_C_COMPILER x86_64-w64-mingw32-gcc)
set(CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER x86_64-w64-mingw32-g++)
set(CMAKE_RC_COMPILER x86_64-w64-mingw32-windres)
set(SF_WIN_SYSROOT "" CACHE PATH "the Windows libraries (an unpacked MSYS2 mingw64 tree)")
set(CMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH /usr/x86_64-w64-mingw32 ${SF_WIN_SYSROOT})
set(CMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH_MODE_PROGRAM NEVER)
set(CMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH_MODE_LIBRARY ONLY)
set(CMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH_MODE_INCLUDE ONLY)
set(CMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH_MODE_PACKAGE ONLY)
# pkg-config (libarchive) from the same tree: its .pc files say /mingw64
if(SF_WIN_SYSROOT)
    get_filename_component(_sf_win_root "${SF_WIN_SYSROOT}" DIRECTORY)
    set(ENV{PKG_CONFIG_LIBDIR} "${SF_WIN_SYSROOT}/lib/pkgconfig")
    set(ENV{PKG_CONFIG_SYSROOT_DIR} "${_sf_win_root}")
endif()

```

`docs/porting.md`:

```md
# Porting the game logic

The game logic is the C of [3s-decomp-ogxb](https://github.com/Manya3084/3s-decomp-ogxb), compiled from a checkout of it (`SF_DECOMP_DIR`, default `../3s-decomp-ogxb`). The checkout is read, never written. `cmake/decomp.cmake` builds it into `libsf_decomp.a`.

The port needs the decomp's `xbox-port-data` branch until it is merged: `color_file` with the Xbox file numbers, the shared structs in place of the files' 32-bit partial copies (`tools/unify_structs.py`), and names for the retail addresses the code used as numbers.

No Xbox code is built. Out of the build:

- `src/sf33rd/Source/XBOX/`: the platform layer (`fl*` texture and palette handles, render states, pads, print, frame end), the Xbox-only game code (the `xb_effect_*_draw` functions, the save menu, the quad renderers, the exit and screenshot screens), and the Xbox Live code.
- `src/cri/`: CRI's Xbox middleware. `src/game/adx.c` and `aix.c` decode natively.
- `Compress/Lz77` and the decomp's zlib copy. The disc uses zlib inflate; the system zlib is linked.
- The XBE. Only its XACT sound banks are read, at import (`src/iso/xbe.c`).

What the game logic calls in those files is written here, in `src/port/`, on SDL3, the Vulkan and OpenGL renderer in `src/render/`, and OpenAL. The Xbox C is the reference for what each call does. It is not compiled.

## Stubs

`tools/gen_stubs.py` runs at link time. Every name the game logic needs that neither `src/port/` nor the host libraries define gets a stub in `build/decomp/stubs.c`:

- a function reports its first call (`port: no shim for NAME`) and returns 0;
- data, meaning any name used other than by a call, is a zeroed 64 KiB block;
- a folded name from the decomp's `tools/fold_aliases.py` jumps to the body it was merged into.

The stub list is the native work left. Defining a name in `src/port/` removes its stub on the next build. `SF_PORT_STRICT=1` aborts on the first stubbed call.

## x86-64

Every address the game code keeps is pointer-sized, so `sf33rdsxb` is an ordinary PIE (on Windows, a relocatable image with high-entropy ASLR), and its memory can be anywhere: `sf_arena_alloc` (`src/port/arena.c`) takes the game's heaps from one block wherever the system puts it. The decomp's `include/` is copied into the build tree with `size_t` and the pointer-sized integers widened. A decomp file that does not compile for x86-64 is listed in `cmake/decomp-overlay.txt` and compiled from a changed copy, a patch from `cmake/decomp-overlay/`. Each such change belongs upstream once the decomp can take it without changing its relink, except the port's own features, listed last in the file: SCREEN ADJUST's three window rows patch `menu.c` (ten rows), `EFF61.c` (their labels), `EFF63.c` (their values come from `display.c`) and `Sel_Data.c` (the rows closer together). Those stay here.

Until October 2026 the program was linked non-PIE at a low address, with its heaps below 2 GB (`MAP_32BIT`), because the decomp kept some addresses in `u32` words: the background and ending rewrite tables, the character files' table offsets turned into addresses in place, a player's effect read as its 203rd word, a table of text pointers read as `s32`s, and the AIX player passed as an `s32`. Those are pointer-sized in the decomp now (`uintptr_t`, which is `u32` on the Xbox, so its i386 objects did not change). The check that found them was a replay of a long recorded session with the heap placed where a truncated address faults (`0x7e00c0000000`), then with the program as a PIE under ASLR; every compared frame matched the low-address build. `clang -Wshorten-64-to-32 -Wint-to-pointer-cast -Wpointer-to-int-cast` over the decomp lists what is left: the size truncations in `bg.c` (sizes, not addresses).

The decomp was written to match the 32-bit build byte for byte, and its 64-bit faults come in three forms:

- a file's own partial copy of a shared struct, padded for the 32-bit layout;
- an address kept in a `u32` table;
- a global declared with one type in one file and another type elsewhere. Both types have the same size on 32-bit and different sizes on x86-64. One case was the effect pool: 69 files indexed it in rows of 0x700 bytes, while it holds 448 pointer-sized words a row.

These are fixed in the decomp itself, and each fix must leave the i386 code and data unchanged. `tools/unify_structs.py` (in the decomp) replaces partial copies with `structs.h`. `tools/lto_type_check.sh BUILD_DIR` lists every global whose declarations disagree. The warnings it still prints are prototypes that differ only in small integer widths, and array bounds.

## What src/port holds

- `gfx.c`: texture and palette handles, the render state call, the quads, the PS2-sized picture's copy. `render/scene.c` draws them on Vulkan at a whole multiple of 640x480 (the window's, or `SF_SCALE`), shown 4:3. Each quad's edges move to the 640x480 pixels D3D would cover, with its texture coordinates recomputed at the moved corners, and each sample is taken at its 640x480 pixel's centre within the quad's texture rectangle: a larger screen shows exactly the pixels 640x480 does, without the seams a finer grid opens between the game's tiles.
- `render/glscene.c`: the same screen on OpenGL 3.3, where Vulkan is missing or `SF_RENDERER=opengl` asks for it: scene.c's passes and shaders (GLSL 330, the texture bound per batch), each framebuffer's rows top first so the numbers match Vulkan's, and the window draw turned over. It gives the same pixels as Vulkan. `SF_HEADLESS=1 SF_RENDERER=opengl` runs it on SDL's offscreen driver for tests.
- `system.c`: the start-up (`njUserInit`), the task loop, the work pool.
- `files.c`: CRI's file calls over `data/afs/SF33RDXB.AFS`.
- `input.c`: the keyboard (port 0) and SDL3 gamepads (ports 1 to 3) through retail's pad conversion and `keyConvert`. Bindings are kept in `config/host.sqlite`.
- `save.c`: the saves, in `data/save/game.sqlite`. A missing database is created from the original defaults. A damaged database is left as it is. `store.c` is the SQLite file.
- `sound.c`: CRI's ADXT and AIXP players on OpenAL streaming sources, fed by `src/game/adx.c` and `aix.c`. Each frame takes 1/60 s of samples from each stream, so the statuses the game waits on (a stem ending) fall on the same frames with or without a device, and a replay stays exact. `SF_SOUND_DEBUG=1` logs the stream calls.
- `se.c`: the sound effects. The game's sound engine (CapSndEng's `cseTsbRequest`) queues requests for retail's XACT layer. `se.c` is that layer: the request queue, 48 voices, group and priority stops, and the pan tables. It plays XACT cues from the sound banks in `data/audio/sdbk`, whose layout is the XDK's `_XACT_SOUNDBANK_*` types, over the wave banks the game loads into memory (`src/game/xwb.c` decodes PCM and Xbox ADPCM). Voices end by the frame count, so effects do not change a replay. `SF_SE_DEBUG=1` logs every effect that starts, and `SF_SE_LFO=0` leaves out the pitch LFO's vibrato.
- `frame.c`, `video.c`: one frame in retail's order; the window, the headless renderer, the 60 fps pacing and the captures.
- `menu_ext.c`: what the port adds to the menus, drawn with the game's pieces (its tone-down, the training menu's red band, its menu font and menu sounds). These are EXIT GAME in place of SELECT GAME (its prompt, and `Reboot_Program` quitting the port) and BUTTON CONFIG's keyboard page (F1). While the page is open the game sees nothing pressed. Also `xb_ToneDownCopy`.
- `display.c`: the window mode, resolution and aspect ratio behind SCREEN ADJUST's rows, kept in `config/host.sqlite` and written when a row changes. FILTER is the game's own row: OFF, SMOOTH, CRT CURVED or CRT FLAT (`sys_w.screen_mode`), drawn when the picture is shown. CRT FLAT is the curved one with square corners. FILTER and the picture size are written to the system database when they change.
- `live.c`: the Xbox Live calls the offline game still makes, answered as offline.
- `zlib.c`, `math.c`, `banks.c`: the game's zlib calls, its D3DX matrix helpers, the sound bank buffers.

The memory manager is `src/game/memman.c`: the decomp's keeps two pointers in a cell header that must fit the 16-byte unit.

## Game state and rollback

Rollback netcode runs frames again from an earlier one, so the game's state has to be saved and put back whole. `src/port/state.c` does that: the state is the program's writable data (the decomp's globals and the generated stubs' blocks) less the port's own (`sfport_data` and `sfport_bss`: the build renames the port libraries' `.data` and `.bss`, cmake/decomp.cmake), plus the used part of the arena (the game's heaps), plus the music streams' place (`sound.c` saves which file each stream plays, where, and its stem queue, and decodes again on a restore). Port files that define the game's own globals (retail kept them in its platform files: `mpp_w`, `plw`, the work pool's ends in `system.c`, the sound settings in `save.c`) put them in the state with `SF_GAME`; `SF_KEEP` keeps the snapshot's own bookkeeping and the input tape out of it.

`SF_ROLLBACK_TEST=FRAME:K` saves at FRAME, runs K frames, puts the state back and runs them again on the same pads (`input.c`'s tape), comparing the state after each; it names the pages that differ. In a fight the frames come out the same (the snapshot is 43 MB, of which a frame changes about 250 KB, so a rollback layer would save changed pages only); across character select and the loads after it they do not yet, as files and textures are made through the port there. `SF_ROLLBACK_ALL=1` takes the port's state in too and `SF_ROLLBACK_EXTRA=ADDR:LEN,...` given ranges (the link map, `build/sf33rdsxb_game.map`, has each file's; the program's CMake target is still `sf33rdsxb_game`), for finding what else the game reads back.

## Threads

The game itself runs on one thread, at a locked 60 frames a second (its own clock, `pace()` in `src/port/video.c`), and everything that thread does each frame decides the game's state, which a replay and rollback need to be exact. What can leave it does, so a slower machine with more cores keeps the 60:

- **The render thread** (`src/port/video.c`). At the end of a frame the game thread waits for the render thread to finish the frame before (it has the rest of a frame to do it), copies the new draw list into the render thread's own (the vertices and batches, and only the textures and palettes whose version changed), and runs on; the render thread draws, presents and waits on the display's refresh. The frame goes to the GPU as soon as it is built. Every other renderer call runs on the render thread between frames, so the renderer is used from one thread (OpenGL's context is handed to it, `sf_render_make_current`). Paced at 60, the hand-off costs the game thread a few microseconds where drawing and presenting took about 80.
- **Workers** (`src/port/jobs.c`): one fewer than the cores, two at most. They read the disc and decode the sound. The game thread asks for a job's result where it needs it and waits only if it is not done, so a job never moves a frame:
  - the game's disc reads (`ADXF_ReadNw` in `src/port/files.c`) run from the request; the status poll, on the next frame, waits for the read;
  - a music stream from the archive reads only its header at its start; a worker reads and decodes the rest in 64 KB parts while it plays (`sf_adx_open`, `sf_adx_step` in `src/game/adx.c`), a queued stem from when it is queued, the opening theme once when the device opens. Up to a sample already decoded, the header's length and loop decide as the decoded ones do, so every status lands on the frame it would with a whole decode;
  - a sound-effect wave bank is decoded when it is registered, and a wave's first play takes it from there;
  - the first start's import, while the window keeps drawing.
- OpenAL Soft mixes and plays on its own thread.

`SF_THREADS=N` sets the workers (0 runs each job on the thread that asks for it, as before there were workers), `SF_RENDER_THREAD=0` draws on the game thread; a one-core machine does both. A replay of a recorded session gives the same frames with and without them.

## Debugging

`SF_SHOT=FRAME:PATH` writes the output after that frame as a PPM (`SF_SHOT_SCREEN=1` the PS2-sized picture before its copy, `2` the layer drawn over it). `SF_SHOT_EVERY=N:DIR` writes one every N frames (between `SF_SHOT_FROM` and `SF_SHOT_TO` when set). `SF_DUMP_QUADS=FRAME` lists the frame's quads (with each one's clamp rectangle), `SF_DUMP_TEX=FRAME` writes its textures to the working directory. `SF_AUTOPRESS=F[:BUTTON],...` holds a button (Start by default) for six frames from each frame F. `SF_HEADLESS=1` runs with no window (Vulkan offscreen; with `SF_SHOT` for captures), `SF_NO_GAMEPADS=1` ignores real pads. Every session's inputs go to `data/last_session.input` (`SF_RECORD=PATH` elsewhere); `SF_REPLAY=PATH` plays a recording back, and the game follows it frame for frame; after its end the game reads the live input again. Headless runs are not paced unless `SF_PACE=1` is set (for capturing audio with OpenAL Soft's wave writer). `SF_KEYPAGE=FRAME` opens the keyboard page on that frame. A crash writes its backtrace to `data/crash.log`. `SF_INPUT_DEBUG=1` prints pad changes. `SF_COUNTRY=1..4` picks the region (4, North America, by default). `SF_SETUP_SOURCE=PATH` gives the first start's screen that source as if it were dropped on it.

## Frame

`src/port/frame.c` is one frame in the order retail's frame function runs it, without the Live calls. `sf_game_init` is its first-frame branch.

```

`packaging/README-linux.txt`:

```txt
SF33RDSXBRecompo: Street Fighter III: 3rd Strike (Xbox), native x86-64
https://github.com/Manya3084/SF33RDSXBRecompo

This package has no game data. You need your own copy of the Xbox disc.

NEEDS
  glibc 2.39 or newer (Ubuntu 24.04, Debian 13, Fedora 40 or later), and:
    Ubuntu/Debian  sudo apt install libopenal1 libarchive13t64 libvulkan1
                   (Debian 12 and older Ubuntu: libarchive13)
    Fedora         sudo dnf install openal-soft libarchive vulkan-loader
    Arch           sudo pacman -S openal libarchive vulkan-icd-loader
  A Vulkan driver, or OpenGL 3.3. SDL3 comes with the package (lib/).

PLAY
  Unpack this folder somewhere you can write to, then run:
    ./sf33rdsxb

  The first time, the window asks for the Xbox disc:
    - click it (or press Enter) and pick the disc image, or a zip or 7z of it;
    - or press F and pick the extracted disc folder (sf33rd.xbe and XB_HostPC);
    - or drop any of those on the window.
  It imports the game into data/ next to the program (about 1.3 GB) and
  starts. After that it starts straight away. A PlayStation 2 disc does not
  work; only the Xbox one.

  The import can also be run from a terminal:
    ./sf33rdsxb --extract /path/to/disc.iso --data .

KEYS (defaults; OPTION > BUTTON CONFIG, F1 rebinds them)
  arrows  d-pad        Z X  A B      A S  X Y      Q  White    E  Black
  W R     L R          Enter Start   Backspace Back
  Gamepads join by pressing Start.

SCREEN
  OPTION > SCREEN ADJUST: window mode, resolution, aspect ratio.
  SF_RENDERER=opengl ./sf33rdsxb uses OpenGL instead of Vulkan.

FILES
  data/                     the imported game
  data/save/game.sqlite    settings, rankings, directions and replays
  config/host.sqlite       the window and the keyboard
  A save from 1.01 (an ini or a .sav) is not read. The first start writes
  the original defaults.

```

`shaders/copy.frag`:

```frag
#version 450
// The screen onto the output: opaque for the PS2-sized picture, alpha
// blended for what is drawn over it.
layout(set = 0, binding = 0) uniform sampler2D screen;
layout(location = 0) in vec2 v_uv;
layout(location = 0) out vec4 o;

void main()
{
    o = texture(screen, v_uv);
}

```

`shaders/scene.frag`:

```frag
#version 450
// The game's fixed-function stage 0: a texture (indexed through a palette,
// or direct), the vertex colour, the colour and alpha ops (D3DTOP numbers)
// with texture as argument 1 and the vertex colour as argument 2, and the
// alpha test. Filtering and addressing are done here so that an indexed
// texture is filtered after its palette lookup.
#ifdef SF_ONE_TEX
// a GPU that cannot index an array of textures in a shader (many phones'):
// the batch's texture is bound on its own, in set 1
layout(set = 1, binding = 0) uniform texture2D tex1;
#define TEX tex1
#else
layout(set = 0, binding = 0) uniform texture2D tex[256];
#define TEX tex[pc.tex]
#endif
layout(set = 0, binding = 1) uniform texture2D pal;
layout(set = 0, binding = 2) uniform sampler smp;

layout(push_constant) uniform Push {
    uint tex;
    uint pal;
    uint ops;   // colour op | alpha op << 8 | address u << 16 | address v << 24
    uint misc;  // point 1, indexed 2, texel uv 4, textured 8, alpha test 16, func << 5, ref << 8
    vec2 size;
    float scale; // screen pixels per 640x480 pixel
} pc;

layout(location = 0) in vec4 v_color;
layout(location = 1) in vec2 v_uv;
layout(location = 2) flat in vec4 v_rect; // the quad's u0, v0, u1, v1
layout(location = 0) out vec4 o;

int address(int i, int n, uint mode)
{
    if (mode == 3u) {
        return clamp(i, 0, n - 1);
    }
    if (mode == 2u) {
        int m = i % (2 * n);
        if (m < 0) {
            m += 2 * n;
        }
        return m < n ? m : 2 * n - 1 - m;
    }
    int w = i % n;
    return w < 0 ? w + n : w;
}

// The texels the quad covers. Drawn 1:1, as retail draws it, every sample
// lands on a texel centre inside them; drawn larger, the edge samples
// would blend in the next cell of the texture's atlas, so taps clamp here.
ivec2 lo;
ivec2 hi;

vec4 fetch(ivec2 t)
{
    ivec2 n = ivec2(pc.size);
    t = clamp(t, lo, hi);
    ivec2 a = ivec2(address(t.x, n.x, (pc.ops >> 16) & 0xFFu), address(t.y, n.y, (pc.ops >> 24) & 0xFFu));
    vec4 c = texelFetch(sampler2D(TEX, smp), a, 0);
    if ((pc.misc & 2u) != 0u) {
        int index = int(c.r * 255.0 + 0.5);
        c = texelFetch(sampler2D(pal, smp), ivec2(index, int(pc.pal)), 0);
    }
    return c;
}

vec4 sample_texture()
{
    vec2 scale = (pc.misc & 4u) != 0u ? vec2(1.0) : pc.size;
    // the texture coordinate at the centre of the 640x480 pixel this one
    // is part of, which is where a 640x480 screen samples it
    vec2 base = (floor(gl_FragCoord.xy / pc.scale) + 0.5) * pc.scale;
    vec2 d = base - gl_FragCoord.xy;
    vec2 uv = v_uv + dFdx(v_uv) * d.x + dFdy(v_uv) * d.y;
    vec2 t = uv * scale;
    vec2 r0 = v_rect.xy * scale;
    vec2 r1 = v_rect.zw * scale;
    lo = ivec2(floor(r0 + 1.0 / 1024.0));
    hi = max(lo, ivec2(ceil(r1 - 1.0 / 1024.0)) - 1);
    if ((pc.misc & 1u) != 0u) {
        // NV2A interpolates in fixed point, so a sample exactly on a texel
        // edge takes the texel after it; float interpolation lands a hair
        // either side, and the bias settles it the hardware's way.
        return fetch(ivec2(floor(t + 1.0 / 1024.0)));
    }
    t -= 0.5;
    ivec2 i = ivec2(floor(t));
    vec2 f = t - floor(t);
    vec4 a = mix(fetch(i), fetch(i + ivec2(1, 0)), f.x);
    vec4 b = mix(fetch(i + ivec2(0, 1)), fetch(i + ivec2(1, 1)), f.x);
    return mix(a, b, f.y);
}

float op1(uint op, float t, float d)
{
    if (op == 2u) return t;
    if (op == 3u) return d;
    if (op == 7u) return min(t + d, 1.0);
    return t * d;
}

vec3 op3(uint op, vec3 t, vec3 d)
{
    if (op == 2u) return t;
    if (op == 3u) return d;
    if (op == 7u) return min(t + d, vec3(1.0));
    return t * d;
}

bool passes(uint func, float a, float ref)
{
    if (func == 0u) return false;
    if (func == 1u) return a < ref;
    if (func == 2u) return a == ref;
    if (func == 3u) return a <= ref;
    if (func == 4u) return a > ref;
    if (func == 5u) return a != ref;
    if (func == 6u) return a >= ref;
    return true;
}

void main()
{
    uint cop = pc.ops & 0xFFu;
    uint aop = (pc.ops >> 8) & 0xFFu;
    vec4 c = v_color;
    if ((pc.misc & 8u) != 0u && cop != 1u) {
        vec4 t = sample_texture();
        c.rgb = op3(cop, t.rgb, v_color.rgb);
        if (aop != 1u) {
            c.a = op1(aop, t.a, v_color.a);
        }
    }
    if ((pc.misc & 16u) != 0u) {
        float a = floor(c.a * 255.0 + 0.5);
        if (!passes((pc.misc >> 5) & 7u, a, float((pc.misc >> 8) & 0xFFu))) {
            discard;
        }
    }
    o = c;
}

```

```vert
#version 450
// A screen-space vertex of the 640x480 game screen. D3D puts pixel centres
// on integer coordinates and Vulkan on half-integers, hence the half pixel.
layout(location = 0) in vec3 pos;
layout(location = 1) in vec4 color;
layout(location = 2) in vec2 uv;
layout(location = 3) in vec4 rect;
layout(location = 0) out vec4 v_color;
layout(location = 1) out vec2 v_uv;
layout(location = 2) flat out vec4 v_rect;

void main()
{
    gl_Position = vec4((pos.x + 0.5) / 320.0 - 1.0, (pos.y + 0.5) / 240.0 - 1.0, pos.z, 1.0);
    v_color = color;
    v_uv = uv;
    v_rect = rect;
}
```

`shaders/show.frag`:

```frag
#version 450
// The game area in the window. mode 0 keeps each texel whole, mode 1
// filters the scale, mode 2 is CRT CURVED and mode 3 is CRT FLAT.
// src is the rectangle the vertex shader maps.
layout(set = 0, binding = 0) uniform sampler2D screen;
layout(push_constant) uniform Push {
    layout(offset = 0) vec4 src;
    layout(offset = 16) float mode;
} pc;
layout(location = 0) in vec2 v_uv;
layout(location = 0) out vec4 o;

vec3 taken(vec2 uv, int sharp)
{
    if (sharp != 0) {
        vec2 size = vec2(textureSize(screen, 0));
        uv = (floor(uv * size) + 0.5) / size;
    }
    return texture(screen, uv).rgb;
}

void main()
{
    int mode = int(pc.mode + 0.5);
    vec2 span;
    vec2 p;
    vec2 uv;
    vec3 col;
    if (mode < 2) {
        o = vec4(taken(v_uv, mode == 0 ? 1 : 0), 1.0);
        return;
    }

    span = pc.src.zw - pc.src.xy;
    p = (v_uv - pc.src.xy) / span;
    if (mode == 2) {
        vec2 c = p * 2.0 - 1.0;
        c *= 1.0 + dot(c, c) * 0.08;
        p = c * 0.5 + 0.5;
        if (p.x < 0.0 || p.y < 0.0 || p.x > 1.0 || p.y > 1.0) {
            o = vec4(0.0, 0.0, 0.0, 1.0);
            return;
        }
    }

    uv = pc.src.xy + span * p;
    col = taken(uv, 0);
    float scan = sin(fract(p.y * 448.0) * 3.14159265);
    col *= mix(0.68, 1.0, scan * scan);
    float mask_x = mod(gl_FragCoord.x, 3.0);
    vec3 mask = mask_x < 1.0 ? vec3(1.0, 0.80, 0.80)
              : mask_x < 2.0 ? vec3(0.80, 1.0, 0.80)
                             : vec3(0.80, 0.80, 1.0);
    col *= mask;
    float vig = smoothstep(1.15, 0.35, length((p - 0.5) * vec2(1.12, 1.0)));
    col *= mix(0.78, 1.0, vig);
    vec2 px = span / vec2(640.0, 448.0);
    vec3 glow = taken(uv + vec2(px.x, 0.0), 0) + taken(uv - vec2(px.x, 0.0), 0) +
                taken(uv + vec2(0.0, px.y), 0) + taken(uv - vec2(0.0, px.y), 0);
    o = vec4(col + glow * 0.03, 1.0);
}

```

`shaders/show.vert`:

```vert
#version 450
// One triangle over the viewport, which is where the screen lands in the window.
layout(push_constant) uniform Push {
    layout(offset = 0) vec4 src; // the screen's x0, y0, x1, y1, normalized
    layout(offset = 20) float rot;
} pc;
layout(location = 0) out vec2 v_uv;

void main()
{
    vec2 p = vec2(float((gl_VertexIndex << 1) & 2), float(gl_VertexIndex & 2));
    gl_Position = vec4(p * 2.0 - 1.0, 0.0, 1.0);
    // the swapchain image's quarter turns (Android's pre-rotation): the
    // texture coordinates turn the other way, so the picture is upright
    int r = int(pc.rot + 0.5);
    vec2 st = r == 1 ? vec2(p.y, 1.0 - p.x) : r == 2 ? vec2(1.0 - p.x, 1.0 - p.y) : r == 3 ? vec2(1.0 - p.y, p.x) : p;
    v_uv = mix(pc.src.xy, pc.src.zw, st);
}
```

```vert
layout(push_constant) uniform Push {
    float rot;
} pc;
layout(location = 0) out vec2 v_uv;

void main() {
    vec2 p = vec2(float((gl_VertexIndex << 1) & 2), float(gl_VertexIndex & 2));
    // the swapchain image's quarter turns (Android's pre-rotation): the
    // texture coordinates turn the other way, so the picture is upright
```
